Page 1 of 173
Regular Session, 2003 
HOUSE BILL NO. 2 
BY REPRESENTATIVES HAMMETT AND LEBLANC AND SENATORS
BARHAM AND DARDENNE
AN ACT
1
To provide with respect to the capital outlay budget and the capital outlay program
2
for state government, state institutions, and other public entities; to provide
3
for the designation of projects and improvements; to provide for the financing
4
thereof making appropriations from certain sources; and to provide for related
5
matters.
6
Be it enacted by the Legislature of Louisiana:
7
Section 1.  The following sums or so much thereof as may be necessary are
8
hereby appropriated out of any monies in the state treasury from the sources
9
specified, from federal funds and self-generated revenues belonging to the state of
10
Louisiana and/or collected by boards, commissions, departments, and agencies
11
thereof, all for making capital outlay and for the purposes and in the amounts
12
specified herein for the Fiscal Year commencing July 1, 2003, and ending June 30,
13
2004, subject to the conditions thereafter provided:
14
A project with funding payable from General Obligation Bonds Priority 1
15
indicates that the funding for  the project includes the reauthorization of funding
16
which was previously on a line of credit granted by the State Bond Commission in
17
Fiscal Year 2002-2003.
18
As used in this Section, the term "general obligation bonds" means state
19
general obligation bonds or other evidences of indebtedness payable from the Bond
20
Security and Redemption Fund.
21
The amount set forth in this Section opposite each project, or so much thereof
22
as may be necessary, is hereby appropriated and payable from the proceeds available
23


ENROLLED
H.B. NO. 2
Page 2 of 173
from the sale of general obligation bonds, unless a different source of funding is
1
indicated.  Where such a different source of funding is indicated, the corresponding
2
amount set forth, or so much thereof as may be necessary, is hereby appropriated and
3
payable from the source of funding indicated; provided, however, amounts
4
appropriated and payable from federal funds may be encumbered or expended only
5
to the extent such amounts shall have been committed or received and amounts for
6
any project payable from revenue bonds in excess of ten million dollars may be
7
encumbered or expended only after approval by the Joint Legislative Committee on
8
Capital Outlay.  Pursuant to the provisions of R.S. 39:82, funds for planning,
9
acquisition, construction, and major repair projects appropriated by this act may be
10
retained until completion of the project, even if no bona fide liability exists on the
11
last day of the fiscal year.
12
This Act shall constitute the comprehensive state capital outlay budget for the
13
2003-2004 Fiscal Year.  Projects set forth which are payable from the proceeds
14
available from the sale of general obligation bonds shall have the priorities as
15
indicated.  Requests for lines of credit must be filed with the Commissioner of
16
Administration.  No general obligation bonds shall be sold, nor cash lines of credit
17
granted to fund any lower priority project prior to the sale of general obligation bonds
18
or the granting of cash lines of credit to fund all higher priority projects (the "general
19
rule"), except as hereinafter provided.  This prohibition against funding a lower
20
priority project shall not apply upon a showing of an impossibility or impracticality
21
either to proceed with all unfunded higher priority projects or to proceed with the
22
funding of such projects or any part thereof through the issuance of general obligation
23
bonds for any reason.  Such showing shall be stated in reasonable detail in a
24
certificate or certificates signed by the state agency responsible for administering the
25
funding, and filed with the Commissioner of Administration and the State Bond
26
Commission (hereinafter referred to as "the commission").  Upon a determination by
27
the commission that proceeding with such a project or the funding thereof through
28
the issuance of general obligation bonds is impractical or impossible, the commission
29
may then proceed with the sale of general obligation bonds or the granting of cash
30


ENROLLED
H.B. NO. 2
Page 3 of 173
lines of credit without regard to the priority of the unfunded projects determined to
1
be impossible or impractical.
2
Upon recommendation by the appropriate person or agency, if it is determined
3
at any time that proceeding with the project or with the funding thereof is no longer
4
impossible or impractical, the State Bond Commission may rescind the certificate of
5
impossibility or impracticality and may proceed with the project or with the issuance
6
of general obligation bonds or the granting of cash lines of credit to fund the project.
7
Alternatively, the Interim Emergency Board (hereinafter referred to as "the
8
board"), upon reviewing certificates as described above and determining that
9
proceeding with such project or the funding thereof through the issuance of general
10
obligation bonds is impossible or impractical, may designate a lower priority for such
11
project.  The board’s determination and lower priority designation shall be submitted
12
to the members of the legislature for their approval in accordance with the provisions
13
of Chapter 3-B of Subtitle I of Title 39 of the Louisiana Revised Statutes of 1950.
14
In accordance with the provisions of Chapter 3-B of Subtitle I of Title 39 of the
15
Louisiana Revised Statutes of 1950, whenever at the written request of the
16
appropriate legislators and agency, the board determines that an adjustment to a
17
project description originally set forth in such capital budget adopted by the
18
legislature for a current fiscal year is necessary to correct a mistake in a project
19
description, clarify a description, change the scope of a description, or make any
20
other adjustment the board deems necessary to implement the purpose of the project,
21
such adjustment shall only become effective upon the approval by majority vote of
22
the elected members of each house of the legislature in the manner provided for in
23
Chapter 3-B of Subtitle I of Title 39 of the Louisiana Revised Statutes of 1950.
24
If such approval is granted, notification by the chairman or secretary of  the
25
board of the results of the balloting shall be transmitted to the State Bond
26
Commission, which may then proceed with the sale of general obligation bonds or
27
the granting of cash lines of credit for each change approved in accordance with the
28
general rule.
29
With respect to Priorities 1 through 5, a lower priority project may be moved
30
to a higher priority only upon a showing of an emergency or other cause not
31


ENROLLED
H.B. NO. 2
Page 4 of 173
reasonably anticipated by the legislature, which shall be stated in reasonable detail
1
in a certificate signed by the head of the appropriate department, political
2
subdivision, or agency and filed with the Commissioner of Administration and the
3
board.  Upon a determination by the board that such project should be moved to a
4
higher priority because of an emergency or other cause not reasonably anticipated by
5
the legislature, the board shall designate a higher priority to the project.  The matter
6
then shall be submitted to and be subject to the same approval by the members of the
7
legislature in the manner previously indicated in this Section.  If such approval is
8
granted, notification by the chairman or secretary of the board of the change of
9
priority shall be transmitted to the State Bond Commission, which then may proceed
10
with the sale of general obligation bonds or the granting of cash lines of credit in
11
accordance with the general rule.  As to projects within a priority, the State Bond
12
Commission shall determine when such project, or phases thereof, shall be funded.
13
In making such determination, the State Bond Commission shall consider the amount
14
of proceeds of general obligation bonds to be made available in light of money and
15
capital market conditions; the expenditure requirements of the project; the time
16
required for architectural plans and drawings, public bidding, and site acquisition and
17
preparation; availability of receipt of federal monies; litigation affecting the
18
undertaking or completion of a project, federal tax laws and regulations regarding the
19
sale of municipal bonds; and other items of a similar nature bearing upon the
20
possibility or the necessity to commence a project or any particular phase thereof. 
21
Any other provision of this Act to the contrary notwithstanding, projects
22
described in Priority 5 of this Act shall not be moved to a higher priority except upon
23
one of the following conditions:
24
(1)  Funding is necessary to complete or additionally fund a project located
25
elsewhere in the capital outlay act.  Determination of such condition shall be made
26
by the head of the appropriate department or agency by means of a certificate to that
27
effect filed with the Interim Emergency Board, who shall, by mail ballot, request
28
approval of such change and if approved by a majority of each house of the
29
legislature it shall be funded, or
30

H.B. NO. 2

(2)  Unless required by an emergency or other cause not reasonably
1
anticipated by the legislature, to be in accordance with procedures set forth in this
2
Section.  However, the State Bond Commission may authorize a noncash line of
3
credit for any project in Priority 5 which represents that portion of construction
4
contracts which were let during a previous fiscal year, or equipment or construction
5
contracts which will be let during the current fiscal year, but which will not require
6
cash expenditures during the year.  Such noncash lines of credit, when combined with
7
previously sold bonds or with higher priority bonds or lines of credit, or with funds
8
appropriated from other sources, will provide sufficient appropriated funds to award
9
equipment or construction contracts.
10
Upon approval by the State Bond Commission, and subject to compliance
11
with its policies and procedures and the procedures set forth herein, a line of credit
12
providing for the use of funds in anticipation of the sale of general obligation bonds
13
may be granted to the appropriate agency or department from the Comprehensive
14
Capital Outlay Escrow Account, for capital improvement projects for which bond
15
proceeds are appropriated by a current capital budget, prior to the actual issuance of
16
bonds for such projects, unless the attorney general advises that an impediment exists
17
to the valid issuance of the bonds on the date such line of credit is granted. 
18
All bonds that are to be repaid, in whole or in part, by revenues generated by
19
the project shall be designated as "Reimbursement Bonds".  The State Bond
20
Commission shall enter into such agreements as are necessary to provide that the
21
agency or subdivision, whose project is reimbursable, shall pay the bond debt costs
22
into the Bond Security and Redemption Fund.  Such costs may be paid annually or
23
at the end of the term of the bonds issued as determined by the State Bond
24
Commission.
25
Priority 1 general obligation bond funding shall be limited to:
26
(1)  Projects and amounts which were previously authorized by Act 23 of the
27
2002 Regular Session of the Legislature; and which were granted cash lines of credit
28
by the State Bond Commission.  The bonds to fund these projects were not sold in
29
Fiscal Year 2002-2003.
30

H.B. NO. 2

(2)  The reauthorization of the portion of the outstanding Fiscal Year 2002-
1
2003 Priority 5 Non-Cash Lines of Credit for projects which will require cash
2
expenditures in Fiscal Year 2003-2004 and therefore must be converted to Cash
3
Lines of Credit in Fiscal Year 2003-2004.
4
(3)  Commitments made in the Higher Education Desegregation Settlement
5
Agreement.
6
Priority 2 general obligation bond projects are for the completion of plans and
7
specifications, land acquisition, site preparation, or for construction which will begin
8
by the first or second quarter of the fiscal year.  It is presently anticipated that the
9
listed projects will require the sale of bonds or extension of lines of credit within six
10
months after adoption of this Act.
11
Priority 3 general obligation bond projects are for the preparation of plans and
12
specifications, land acquisition, site preparation, or for construction which will begin
13
in the third quarter of the fiscal year.  It is presently anticipated that the listed projects
14
will require the sale of bonds or extension of lines of credit during the current fiscal
15
year.
16
Priority 4 general obligation bond projects are for the preparation of plans and
17
specifications, land acquisition, site preparation, or for construction.  It is presently
18
anticipated that the listed projects will require the sale of bonds or extension of lines
19
of credit during the fiscal year if all higher priority projects are funded or declared
20
impossible or impractical.
21
Priority 5 general obligation bond funding shall be limited to funding for the
22
portion of construction contracts or equipment purchases which will be bid and
23
awarded during the fiscal year, but which will not require cash expenditures during
24
the fiscal year.  These funds, when combined with previously sold bonds or with
25
higher priority bonds on lines of credit, or with funds appropriated from other
26
sources, will provide sufficient appropriated funds to award contracts.  Non-Cash
27
lines of credit shall be required prior to the award of these contracts.
28
In accordance with the cash management plan adopted pursuant to R.S.

30
